From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what finally pushed me to make an appointment is that I just keep getting up when I really shouldn't. Like, during sprint planning at work, or... or even on important personal things, like mortgage calls. I know I should stay put and focus, but I’ll suddenly feel like I absolutely need to go get water, or I’ll just decide to go check something in the other room right in the middle of it. It's just getting a bit frustrating to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're making these decisions to get up and leave on a whim, without really thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it that way. I guess... I wouldn't even really call it a decision. It's more like I just find myself standing up before I've actually processed that I'm doing it. So I definitely don't think it through, but I don't know if it's exactly on a whim, either. It just sort of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. In meetings, I'm almost always bouncing my knee or clicking a pen. Or I'll just keep shifting around in my chair. I usually don't even notice how much I'm doing it until someone looks over at me.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties with sitting still and leaving tasks unfinished causing problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. It's started creating some real friction, honestly. At work, and at home too. People are having to follow up with me a lot more than they used to because I just... drift away from things. I really worry that I'm becoming the unreliable one, especially when it comes to planning out tasks or getting things organized. And the frustrating part is, I'm trying really hard. It just doesn't look like it to anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse, doing things without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... like I said about getting up from my desk, that happens without thinking. But as far as making actual decisions or doing things without considering the consequences, I don't think I really do that. I'm not out there taking big risks or making major choices on a whim or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It seems to happen a lot when there are a few steps involved. Like... if my husband asks me to call the insurance company, upload a form, and then email the confirmation. I'll usually do the first part right away, but somehow that last step just sits unfinished. I won't even realize I didn't complete the whole process until he reminds me about it later.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I know I get restless, like I mentioned, but actual distractions around me don't bother me too much. When I'm looking at my screens for work, I can usually tune out background noise or people walking by just fine. So I wouldn't say I get pulled away by things happening in the room... it's more just me feeling the need to get up and move on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. With my job—I write software—I kind of have to be pretty focused on the details. If I make a careless mistake, the code just doesn't work, so I'm used to checking things closely. So... no, I wouldn't say I struggle with that. I'm actually pretty good at catching the small things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you get up and leave or feel restless when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's tied to feeling stressed or overwhelmed. It's more of a physical thing, honestly. Like, I'll just be sitting there working, completely fine, not anxious at all, and I just get this urge that I need to stand up or walk around. It's not a reaction to my mood or my workload... it's just my body wanting to move.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these difficulties with restlessness and completing tasks?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's been noticeably harder to manage for about two years now. That's right around when we took on our mortgage. It happens even when I'm working on a project I actually find interesting. So I haven't really noticed it being tied to boredom, either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant impact of your restlessness and difficulty completing tasks on your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I know I should stay put and focus, but I’ll suddenly feel like I absolutely need to go get water, or I’ll just decide to go check something in the other room right in the middle of it. It's just getting a bit frustrating to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're making these decisions to get up and leave on a whim, without really thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it that way. I guess... I wouldn't even really call it a decision. It's more like I just find myself standing up before I've actually processed that I'm doing it. So I definitely don't think it through, but I don't know if it's exactly on a whim, either. It just sort of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. In meetings, I'm almost always bouncing my knee or clicking a pen. Or I'll just keep shifting around in my chair. It's started creating some real friction, honestly. At work, and at home too. People are having to follow up with me a lot more than they used to because I just... drift away from things. I really worry that I'm becoming the unreliable one, especially when it comes to planning out tasks or getting things organized. And the frustrating part is, I'm trying really hard. It just doesn't look like it to anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse, doing things without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... like I said about getting up from my desk, that happens without thinking. But as far as making actual decisions or doing things without considering the consequences, I don't think I really do that. I'm not out there taking big risks or making major choices on a whim or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It seems to happen a lot when there are a few steps involved. Like... if my husband asks me to call the insurance company, upload a form, and then email the confirmation. I'll usually do the first part right away, but somehow that last step just sits unfinished. I won't even realize I didn't complete the whole process until he reminds me about it later.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I know I get restless, like I mentioned, but actual distractions around me don't bother me too much. When I'm looking at my screens for work, I can usually tune out background noise or people walking by just fine. So I wouldn't say I get pulled away by things happening in the room... it's more just me feeling the need to get up and move on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. With my job—I write software—I kind of have to be pretty focused on the details. If I make a careless mistake, the code just doesn't work, so I'm used to checking things closely. So... no, I wouldn't say I struggle with that. It's not a reaction to my mood or my workload... it's just my body wanting to move.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these difficulties with restlessness and completing tasks?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's been noticeably harder to manage for about two years now. That's right around when we took on our mortgage. It's just getting a bit frustrating to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're making these decisions to get up and leave on a whim, without really thinking them through?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it that way. I guess... I wouldn't even really call it a decision. It's more like I just find myself standing up before I've actually processed that I'm doing it. So I definitely don't think it through, but I don't know if it's exactly on a whim, either. It just sort of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. In meetings, I'm almost always bouncing my knee or clicking a pen. Or I'll just keep shifting around in my chair. I usually don't even notice how much I'm doing it until someone looks over at me.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties with sitting still and leaving tasks unfinished causing problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. It's started creating some real friction, honestly. At work, and at home too. People are having to follow up with me a lot more than they used to because I just... drift away from things. I really worry that I'm becoming the unreliable one, especially when it comes to planning out tasks or getting things organized. And the frustrating part is, I'm trying really hard. It just doesn't look like it to anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse, doing things without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... like I said about getting up from my desk, that happens without thinking. But as far as making actual decisions or doing things without considering the consequences, I don't think I really do that. I'm not out there taking big risks or making major choices on a whim or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects that you start?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It seems to happen a lot when there are a few steps involved. Like... if my husband asks me to call the insurance company, upload a form, and then email the confirmation. I'll usually do the first part right away, but somehow that last step just sits unfinished. I won't even realize I didn't complete the whole process until he reminds me about it later.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I know I get restless, like I mentioned, but actual distractions around me don't bother me too much. When I'm looking at my screens for work, I can usually tune out background noise or people walking by just fine. So I wouldn't say I get pulled away by things happening in the room... it's more just me feeling the need to get up and move on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. With my job—I write software—I kind of have to be pretty focused on the details. If I make a careless mistake, the code just doesn't work, so I'm used to checking things closely. So... no, I wouldn't say I struggle with that. I'm actually pretty good at catching the small things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you get up and leave or feel restless when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's tied to feeling stressed or overwhelmed. It's more of a physical thing, honestly. Like, I'll just be sitting there working, completely fine, not anxious at all, and I just get this urge that I need to stand up or walk around. It's not a reaction to my mood or my workload... it's just my body wanting to move.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these difficulties with restlessness and completing tasks?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's been noticeably harder to manage for about two years now. That's right around when we took on our mortgage. There was just all this extra paperwork and planning that came with buying the house, and... yeah, that's when I really started noticing the restlessness becoming more of an issue when I needed to sit down and get things done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness or tendency to get up and leave tasks is ever triggered by feeling bored?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really think so. It happens even when I'm working on a project I actually find interesting. Like, I can be totally engaged in writing a piece of code, not bored at all, and I'll still get that... that feeling where I just need to get up and stretch my legs. So I haven't really noticed it being tied to boredom, either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant impact of your restlessness and difficulty completing tasks on your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's actually started creating some friction, both at home and at work. It seems like... well, people just have to follow up with me a lot more than they used to. I really worry that I'm becoming the unreliable one, especially when it comes to planning tasks. I mean, I'm trying really hard, but it's definitely causing some frustration for my family and the people I work with.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any of these difficulties with restlessness and completing tasks when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I actually remember my teachers in elementary school having to move my desk around, or... or constantly reminding me to just stay in my seat. I was always popping up out of my chair, or talking before I was called on. And I would rush through my assignments, even when I completely understood the work. So, yes, it was definitely an issue back then, too.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant periods of time since childhood where these symptoms of restlessness and difficulty completing tasks were not present?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think there's ever been a time where it just stopped. It's... it's pretty much always been there. I mean, maybe there were phases in my life where I managed it a little better, or my routine made it less obvious, but the actual feeling of being restless and having trouble finishing things... that hasn't ever really gone away since I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost a job, had a relationship end, or experienced other significant life problems because of your restlessness and difficulty completing tasks?\n\nPatient: No, thankfully nothing that extreme. I haven't been fired or had a major breakup over it. It's more just... like I said, it causes friction and frustration. People get annoyed with me, and I get annoyed with myself, but it hasn't actually ruined a relationship or cost me my job. I've always managed to scrape by and get things done eventually, even if it's stressful for everyone involved.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I haven't been fired or had a major breakup over it. It's more just... like I said, it causes friction and frustration. People get annoyed with me, and I get annoyed with myself, but it hasn't actually ruined a relationship or cost me my job. I've always managed to scrape by and get things done eventually, even if it's stressful for everyone involved.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I've never really noticed that being an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms of restlessness and difficulty completing tasks significantly impacted your ability to perform daily activities?\n\nPatient: It definitely impacts things, yeah. I really worry that I'm becoming the unreliable one when it comes to planning tasks. I haven't had to stop doing anything. I mean, I still do all my normal daily stuff... going to work, running errands, things like that. I haven't made any major changes to my routine.
